I don’t want to make it sound overly dramatic, but it’s started to scare me a little—like walking out in the middle of work deployments, or driving around really late at night with no plan. I’ve always handled stress pretty responsibly, so… this feels off, and I thought I should talk to someone before it gets worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re not enjoying things as much as you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you once found pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, a little. Lately I’ll say I’m tired or something came up, and I cancel. Then I just sit at home scrolling on my phone or put on a show I’m not really watching.\n\nIt sounds bad when I say it out loud. I don’t know if that’s what you mean, but… yes, I’ve noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods where you felt unusually high, extremely irritable, or so energized that you didn’t need much sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. I mean, I still get through work and everything, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I’m tired a lot.\n\nMost nights I’m lying there replaying parts of what happened at the office, and then when I finally do fall asleep, I’ll wake up tense—from a dream or even just some little noise. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way—losing interest in things, feeling tired, and having trouble sleeping—for at least two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely more than two weeks. I wish it were just that.\n\nIt’s been going on since that late-night cutover incident at work… a little over a year ago now, maybe around 13 months. Some weeks are better than others, so I don’t want to overstate it, but it hasn’t really gone away.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you’re a failure or that you’ve let people down, or been really hard on yourself about things lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to sit around thinking I’m a failure or anything, but I do get really hard on myself.\n\nEven on quiet days, there’s this mix of fear and shame just kind of sitting in my chest. I know that probably sounds a little dramatic, but that’s sort of how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Have you had episodes like this before, or is this the first time you’ve felt this way over an extended period?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Is that the kind of distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in the past where you felt down, lost interest in things, or had trouble sleeping for more than a couple of weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. Is that okay as an answer?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. there and I just can’t pull it back.\n- key: S074\n  name: Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State\n  description: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).\n  manifestation: Even on quiet days I feel this mix of fear and shame sitting in my chest, like I should have handled everything better and like something could still happen if I let my guard down.\n- key: S076\n  name: Inability_Positive_Emotions\n  description: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).\n  manifestation: My husband will hug me or tell me he’s proud of me, and I know I love him in my head, but the warm feeling I used to get just doesn’t come through.\n- key: S078\n  name: Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior\n  description: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.\n  manifestation: I came in because I’ve started doing things that scare me, like walking out in the middle of deployments or driving around late at night with no plan, and that is not how I’ve ever handled stress before.\n- key: S080\n  name: traumatic event.\n  manifestation: I take the long way around the building, avoid that floor whenever I can, and I shut down conversations the moment people start talking about the contractor or the
